# Step 1: Filter customers with at least 5 transactions and calculate total and successful transaction counts.
# We assume a transaction is 'successful' if its status is 'completed'.
customer_metrics = Customers.WHERE(COUNT(transactions_made) >= 5).CALCULATE(
    cust_name=name,
    num_total_transactions=COUNT(transactions_made),
    num_successful_transactions=COUNT(transactions_made.WHERE(status == "completed"))
)

# Step 2: Calculate the success rate from the metrics obtained in Step 1.
# Select the customer name and their success rate, then order by success rate ascending.
transaction_success_rates = customer_metrics.CALCULATE(
    customer_name=cust_name,
    success_rate=IFF(num_total_transactions > 0, (num_successful_transactions * 100.0) / num_total_transactions, 0.0)
).ORDER_BY(success_rate.ASC())

# Define the list of FAANG ticker symbols as a contextless expression
faang_tickers = ("AMZN", "AAPL", "GOOGL", "META", "NFLX")

# Filter customers based on email and their transactions
# 1. Filter customers whose email ends with ".com"
# 2. Check if these customers have made any transactions (transactions_made)
# 3. For those transactions, check if the ticker symbol (transactions_made.ticker.symbol)
#    is in the faang_tickers list using ISIN.
# 4. Use HAS to ensure at least one such transaction exists for the customer.
customers_meeting_criteria = Customers.WHERE(
    ENDSWITH(email, ".com") &
    HAS(transactions_made.WHERE(ISIN(ticker.symbol, faang_tickers))) == 1
)

# Count the number of such distinct customers
# The result is calculated at the Broker (top-level graph) scope.
result = Broker.CALCULATE(
    num_distinct_customers = COUNT(customers_meeting_criteria)
)

# Step 1: Filter customers who meet the TAC criteria.
# TAC are defined as customers who joined on or after January 1, 2023, and have a status of "Active".
filtered_tac_customers = Customers.WHERE(
    (join_date >= "2023-01-01") & (status == "Active")
)

# Step 2: Partition the filtered TAC by country and then calculate the count for each country.
# The 'country' field from the Customers collection is used for partitioning.
# For each country, we count the number of customers belonging to that partition.
result = filtered_tac_customers.PARTITION(name="country_groups", by=country).CALCULATE(
    customer_country=country,  # The country from the partition key
    tac_count=COUNT(Customers) # Counts the customers within each country group
)

[DEBUG] [Q4] Running model claude (attempt 0)
[DEBUG] [Q4] Running model gemini (attempt 0)
[ERROR] [Q4] Model claude failed on attempt 0: [ClaudeAIProvider] Request failed: Streaming is strongly recommended for operations that may take longer than 10 minutes. See https://github.com/anthropics/anthropic-sdk-python#long-requests for more details
[DEBUG] [Q4] Code generated by gemini:
# Define the start and end dates for the 8-week period, excluding the current week.
# The period starts 8 weeks before the start of the current week.
# The period ends at the start of the current week (making it exclusive of the current week).
start_period_boundary = DATETIME('now', 'start of week', '-8 weeks')
end_period_boundary = DATETIME('now', 'start of week')

# Filter transactions to include only those for 'Stock' tickers
# and those that occurred within the defined 8-week period.
relevant_stock_transactions = Transactions.WHERE(
    (ticker.ticker_type == 'Stock') &
    (date_time >= start_period_boundary) &
    (date_time < end_period_boundary)
)

# For each relevant transaction, calculate the start date of the week it belongs to
# and determine if the transaction occurred on a weekend (Saturday or Sunday).
transactions_enriched_with_week_data = relevant_stock_transactions.CALCULATE(
    # New fields are calculated based on existing data from relevant_stock_transactions:
    week_start_date_calc = DATETIME(date_time, 'start of week'),
    is_weekend_transaction_flag = ISIN(DAYNAME(date_time), ("Saturday", "Sunday"))
)

# Group these enriched transactions by the calculated week_start_date_calc.
# Then, for each week, calculate the total number of stock transactions
# and the number of those that occurred on a weekend.
weekly_transaction_summary = transactions_enriched_with_week_data.PARTITION(
    name="txns_per_week",  # Name for the partition group
    by=(week_start_date_calc)  # Partition by the calculated week start date
).CALCULATE(
    # Output fields for each week:
    week_start = week_start_date_calc,  # The week identifier (from the partition key)
    total_transactions = COUNT(transactions_enriched_with_week_data),  # Total stock transactions in this week's group
    weekend_transactions = SUM(IFF(transactions_enriched_with_week_data.is_weekend_transaction_flag, 1, 0))  # Sum of weekend transactions (where flag is true)
).ORDER_BY(week_start.ASC()) # Order results chronologically by the week's start date.

# Assign the final result to the 'result' variable.
result = weekly_transaction_summary

# Define the start and end of the analysis period:
# last 8 weeks, excluding the current week.
# eight_weeks_ago_start is the beginning of the 8th week before the current week.
eight_weeks_ago_start = DATETIME('now', 'start of week', '-56 days')
# current_week_start_exclusive_end is the beginning of the current week,
# serving as an exclusive end boundary for our period.
current_week_start_exclusive_end = DATETIME('now', 'start of week')

# 1. Filter transactions to include only those for stocks within the desired period,
#    and calculate necessary week-related information.
transactions_info = Transactions.WHERE(
    (ticker.ticker_type == 'Stock') &  # Filter for stock transactions
    (date_time >= eight_weeks_ago_start) &  # Transaction occurred on or after the start of the 8-week period
    (date_time < current_week_start_exclusive_end)  # Transaction occurred before the start of the current week
).CALCULATE(
    # Determine the start date of the week for each transaction
    transaction_week_start = DATETIME(date_time, 'start of week'),
    # Flag weekend transactions (Saturday or Sunday)
    is_weekend = ISIN(DAYNAME(date_time), ("Saturday", "Sunday"))
)

# 2. Group the filtered and augmented transactions by the week's start date.
#    Then, calculate the total stock transactions and weekend stock transactions for each week.
weekly_stock_transactions_summary = transactions_info.PARTITION(
    name="weekly_group",  # Name for the partition group
    by=(transaction_week_start)  # Group by the calculated week start date
).CALCULATE(
    week_starting_on = transaction_week_start,  # Output the week start date
    total_transactions_for_stocks = COUNT(Transactions),  # Count all stock transactions in this week group
    weekend_transactions_for_stocks = SUM(IFF(transactions_info.is_weekend, 1, 0))  # Sum flags for weekend transactions
).ORDER_BY(week_starting_on.ASC())  # Order results by week, oldest first

# Assign the final result to the 'result' variable
result = weekly_stock_transactions_summary

# Define the start of the current week
start_of_current_week_calc = DATETIME('now', 'start of week')
# Define the start of last week (7 days before the start of the current week)
start_of_last_week_calc = DATETIME(start_of_current_week_calc, '-7 days')

# Identify transactions made by customers from the USA within the last week
# A transaction is in "last week" if its date_time is on or after the start of last week
# AND before the start of the current week.
relevant_transactions = Customers.WHERE(country == "USA").transactions_made.WHERE(
    (date_time >= start_of_last_week_calc) &
    (date_time < start_of_current_week_calc)
)

# Calculate the total number of these transactions and their total amount
# The result is a single row with two columns: num_transactions and total_transaction_amount
result = Broker.CALCULATE(
    num_transactions = COUNT(relevant_transactions),
    total_transaction_amount = SUM(relevant_transactions.amount)
)

# Define the start and end boundaries for "last week"
# 'start of week' truncates the current timestamp to the beginning of the current week.
# Subtracting 7 days gives the beginning of the previous week.
start_of_last_week_dt = DATETIME('now', 'start of week', '-7 days')
# The end of "last week" is the beginning of the current week.
end_of_last_week_dt = DATETIME('now', 'start of week')

# First, filter customers from the USA
usa_customers = Customers.WHERE(country == "USA")

# Then, access their transactions and filter those that occurred last week
last_week_usa_transactions = usa_customers.transactions_made.WHERE(
    (date_time >= start_of_last_week_dt) & (date_time < end_of_last_week_dt)
)

# Finally, calculate the number of transactions and their total amount
result = Broker.CALCULATE(
    num_of_transactions = COUNT(last_week_usa_transactions),
    total_trans_amount = SUM(last_week_usa_transactions.amount)
)

# Define the start of the current week and the start of the last week
# 'start of week' truncates the current timestamp to the beginning of the week (e.g., Monday 00:00:00)
# '-7 days' subtracts 7 days, effectively giving the start of the previous week.
start_of_current_week_val = DATETIME('now', 'start of week')
start_of_last_week_val = DATETIME('now', 'start of week', '-7 days')

# Filter transactions:
# 1. The customer associated with the transaction must be from the "USA".
# 2. The transaction's date_time must be on or after the start of last week
#    AND before the start of the current week.
filtered_transactions_data = Transactions.WHERE(
    (customer.country == "USA") &
    (date_time >= start_of_last_week_val) &
    (date_time < start_of_current_week_val)
)

# Calculate the number of transactions and the total transaction amount
# from the filtered transactions.
# The results are presented as fields in the top-level Broker collection.
last_week_usa_summary = Broker.CALCULATE(
    num_transactions=COUNT(filtered_transactions_data),
    total_transaction_amount=SUM(filtered_transactions_data.amount)

# Step 1: Filter transactions for "buy" type and within the last 10 days,
# and extract the ticker symbol.
# DATETIME('now', '-10 days') creates a timestamp 10 days prior to the current moment.
# So, date_time >= DATETIME('now', '-10 days') filters for transactions in the last 10-day window.
# ticker.symbol accesses the symbol from the Tickers record related to the transaction.
filtered_transactions_with_symbol = Transactions.WHERE(
    (transaction_type == "buy") &
    (date_time >= DATETIME('now', '-10 days'))
).CALCULATE(
    ticker_sym = ticker.symbol  # Extracting ticker symbol
)

# Step 2: Partition by the extracted ticker symbol and count the number of buy transactions
# for each symbol.
# 'ticker_sym' is used as the partition key.
# COUNT(Transactions) counts the number of original Transaction records
# that fall into each partition group (i.e., have the same ticker_sym and passed the initial filters).
ticker_buy_counts = filtered_transactions_with_symbol.PARTITION(
    name="ticker_groups", by=(ticker_sym)
).CALCULATE(
    ticker_symbol_value = ticker_sym,  # The ticker symbol from the partition key
    num_buy_transactions = COUNT(Transactions)  # Count of buy transactions for this symbol
)

# Step 3: Select the top 2 ticker symbols based on the number of buy transactions.
# The results are ordered by num_buy_transactions in descending order.
top_2_frequent_tickers = ticker_buy_counts.TOP_K(
    2, by=num_buy_transactions.DESC()
)

# Step 4: Format the final output to return only the ticker symbol and the count.
# Renaming 'ticker_symbol_value' to 'ticker_symbol' for the final output.
result = top_2_frequent_tickers.CALCULATE(
    ticker_symbol = ticker_symbol_value,
    num_buy_transactions = num_buy_transactions
)

# Define the reporting period: last 6 full months, excluding the current month.
# _current_month_start is the first day of the current month.
# _report_start_date is the first day of the month 6 months prior to the current month.
# _report_end_date is the last day of the month immediately preceding the current month.
date_info = Broker.CALCULATE(
    _current_month_start = DATETIME('now', 'start of month')
).CALCULATE(
    _report_start_date = DATETIME(_current_month_start, '-6 months'),
    _report_end_date = DATETIME(_current_month_start, '-1 day')
)

# Filter customers who joined within this defined reporting period.
# For each of these customers, create a string representation of their signup year and month (e.g., "2023-01").
# LPAD is used to ensure months are two digits (e.g., "01" for January) for consistent sorting and grouping.
customers_for_stats = date_info.Customers.WHERE(
    (join_date >= _report_start_date) & (join_date <= _report_end_date)
).CALCULATE(
    signup_year_month_str = JOIN_STRINGS(
        "-",
        YEAR(join_date),
        LPAD(MONTH(join_date), 2, "0")
    )
)

# Partition the filtered customers by their signup year and month.
# For each month in the reporting period:
# - PMCS (Per Month Customer Signups): Count the number of customers who signed up in that month.
# - PMAT (Per Month Average Transaction Amount): Calculate the average transaction amount
#   for all transactions made by customers who signed up in that month.
# The results are ordered by the signup year and month.
result = customers_for_stats.PARTITION(
    name="monthly_signup_group", by=(signup_year_month_str)
).CALCULATE(
    year_month_of_signup = signup_year_month_str,
    PMCS = COUNT(Customers),
    PMAT = AVG(Customers.transactions_made.amount)
).ORDER_BY(year_month_of_signup.ASC())

# Define date boundaries for the last 6 months, excluding the current month.
# 'current_month_start_val' is the first day of the current month.
current_month_start_val = DATETIME('now', 'start of month')
# 'six_months_ago_start_val' is the first day of the month, 6 months prior to the current month.
six_months_ago_start_val = DATETIME('now', 'start of month', '-6 months')

# Filter customers who signed up within the defined period.
# Also, create a composite key 'signup_year_month_partition_key' (e.g., "YYYY-MM")
# from their join_date for monthly partitioning.
customers_for_metrics = Broker.Customers.WHERE(
    (join_date >= six_months_ago_start_val) & (join_date < current_month_start_val)
).CALCULATE(
    signup_year_month_partition_key = JOIN_STRINGS(
        "-", # Delimiter
        YEAR(join_date), # Year of signup
        LPAD(MONTH(join_date), 2, "0") # Month of signup, padded with leading zero if needed
    )
    # The 'transactions_made' sub-collection is implicitly available for these customers.
)

# Partition the filtered customers by their signup month (using the generated key).
# Then, for each monthly cohort, calculate PMCS and PMAT.
result = customers_for_metrics.PARTITION(
    name="monthly_cohorts", by=(signup_year_month_partition_key)
).CALCULATE(
    signup_period = signup_year_month_partition_key, # Display the signup year-month
    PMCS = COUNT(Broker.Customers), # PMCS: Count of customers who signed up in this 'signup_period'.
                                    # 'Broker.Customers' here refers to the customers within the current partition group.
    PMAT = AVG(Broker.Customers.transactions_made.amount) # PMAT: Average transaction amount for customers who signed up
                                                          # in this 'signup_period'.
).ORDER_BY(signup_period.ASC()) # Order the results chronologically by signup period.

).WHERE(
    # Filter for records from the last 7 days, inclusive of today

# DATEDIFF("days", date, 'now') <= 6 covers today (diff=0) and the 6 preceding days.

# 'ticker.ticker_type' accesses the type from the related Ticker record.

    extracted_ticker_type = ticker.ticker_type
)

# Step 3: Partition the data by the 'extracted_ticker_type'.

    by=(extracted_ticker_type)

# 'extracted_ticker_type' is the partition key.

[DEBUG] [Q10] Running model claude (attempt 0)

[ERROR] [Q10] Model claude failed on attempt 0: [ClaudeAIProvider] Request failed: Streaming is strongly recommended for operations that may take longer than 10 minutes. See https://github.com/anthropics/anthropic-sdk-python#long-requests for more details
[DEBUG] [Q10] Code generated by gemini:
# Step 1 & 2: Filter customers who joined in 2022 and prepare necessary fields for AR calculation.
customers_data_for_ar = Customers.WHERE(YEAR(join_date) == 2022).CALCULATE(
    customer_country=country,  # To be used for partitioning
    customer_status=status,    # To check if a customer is 'Active'
    customer_has_transactions=(HAS(transactions_made) == 1) # True if the customer has at least one transaction
)

# Step 3 & 4: Partition by country and calculate AR for each country.
activity_ratio_by_country = customers_data_for_ar.PARTITION(
    name="country_partition", by=(customer_country) # Group customers by their country
).CALCULATE(
    output_country=customer_country, # The country from the partition key

    # Numerator for AR: Count of customers in this country (joined 2022)
    # who are 'Active' AND have transactions.
    # 'Customers' here refers to the 'customers_data_for_ar' records within the current 'country_partition'.
    active_customers_with_txns_count=COUNT(
        Customers.WHERE((customer_status == "Active") & (customer_has_transactions == True))
    ),

    # Denominator for AR: Count of customers in this country (joined 2022)
    # who have transactions.
    total_customers_with_txns_count=COUNT(
        Customers.WHERE(customer_has_transactions == True)
    ),

    # Calculate AR. If total_customers_with_txns_count is 0, AR is 0.0 to avoid division by zero.
    calculated_ar=IFF(
        COUNT(Customers.WHERE(customer_has_transactions == True)) > 0, # Check if denominator is non-zero
        (COUNT(Customers.WHERE((customer_status == "Active") & (customer_has_transactions == True))) * 100.0) / COUNT(Customers.WHERE(customer_has_transactions == True)),
        0.0
    )
# Step 5: Select and rename final fields for the output.
).CALCULATE(country=output_country, AR=calculated_ar)

# Step 1 & 2: Filter customers by join year and calculate necessary flags
customers_prepared = Customers.WHERE(YEAR(join_date) == 2022).CALCULATE(
    customer_country_field = country,
    is_customer_active_status = (status == "Active"), # Assuming 'Active' is the literal string for active status
    customer_has_transactions = (HAS(transactions_made) == 1)
)

# Step 3: Filter for customers who form the base for AR calculation (must have transactions)
customers_for_ar_base = customers_prepared.WHERE(customer_has_transactions == 1)

# Step 4 & 5: Partition by country and calculate AR
country_ar_calculation = customers_for_ar_base.PARTITION(name="country_groups", by=(customer_country_field)).CALCULATE(
    output_country = customer_country_field,
    count_total_with_txn = COUNT(Customers), # Denominator: Total customers in this group (all have transactions)
    count_active_with_txn = COUNT(Customers.WHERE(is_customer_active_status == 1)), # Numerator: Count of 'Active' customers among them
    output_ar_value = IFF(
        count_total_with_txn > 0,
        (100.0 * count_active_with_txn) / count_total_with_txn,
        0.0 # AR is 0 if no customers with transactions, though partitioning on customers_for_ar_base should prevent count_total_with_txn being 0.
    )
)

# Step 6: Final selection of fields to match the requested output "country and AR"
final_result = country_ar_calculation.CALCULATE(country=output_country, AR=output_ar_value)

# Step 1: Filter customers who joined in 2022 and have at least one transaction.
customers_joined_2022_with_transactions = Customers.WHERE(
    (YEAR(join_date) == 2022) & (HAS(transactions_made) == 1)
)

# Step 2: Partition these customers by country and calculate the AR.
# Within each partition (country):
# - COUNT(Customers) gives the total number of customers who joined in 2022 and have transactions for that country.
# - COUNT(Customers.WHERE(status == "Active")) gives the number of active customers
#   who joined in 2022 and have transactions for that country.
activity_ratio_by_country = customers_joined_2022_with_transactions.PARTITION(
    name="country_groups", by=(country)
).CALCULATE(
    country_name=country,
    # AR = (Number of Active Customers with Transactions / Total Number of Customers with Transactions) * 100
    AR=IFF(
        COUNT(Customers) > 0,  # This is the Total Number of Customers with Transactions for the country
        (100.0 * COUNT(Customers.WHERE(status == "Active"))) / COUNT(Customers),
        0.0  # Default AR to 0.0 if there are no customers with transactions in this group
    )
)

# Step 3: Select only the country and AR for the final result.
result = activity_ratio_by_country.CALCULATE(country=country_name, AR=AR)

[DEBUG] [Q12] Running model claude (attempt 0)
[DEBUG] [Q12] Running model gemini (attempt 0)
[ERROR] [Q12] Model claude failed on attempt 0: [ClaudeAIProvider] Request failed: Streaming is strongly recommended for operations that may take longer than 10 minutes. See https://github.com/anthropics/anthropic-sdk-python#long-requests for more details

# Define the date range for the "past month, inclusive of 1 month ago"
one_month_ago_date = DATETIME('now', '-1 month')
current_time = DATETIME('now')

# Step 1: Filter transactions for "sell" type, within the date range,
# and calculate necessary attributes including the ticker symbol.
transactions_for_spm_calculation = Transactions.WHERE(
    (transaction_type == "sell") &
    (date_time >= one_month_ago_date) &
    (date_time <= current_time)
).CALCULATE(
    # Extract ticker symbol via the 'ticker' relationship
    ticker_symbol_value=ticker.symbol,
    # Extract relevant financial figures for SPM calculation
    sell_amount_value=amount,
    tax_value=tax,
    commission_value=commission
)

# Step 2: Partition the filtered transactions by ticker symbol.
# Then, for each ticker symbol, aggregate the total sell amount, tax, and commission.
aggregated_by_ticker = transactions_for_spm_calculation.PARTITION(
    name="transactions_per_ticker_group", by=(ticker_symbol_value)
).CALCULATE(
    # The partition key (ticker symbol)
    ticker_symbol=ticker_symbol_value,
    # Summing up values for each ticker group.
    # 'Transactions' here refers to the items from 'transactions_for_spm_calculation'
    # that fall into the current partition group.
    total_sell_amount_for_ticker=SUM(Transactions.sell_amount_value),
    total_tax_for_ticker=SUM(Transactions.tax_value),
    total_commission_for_ticker=SUM(Transactions.commission_value)
)

# Step 3: Calculate SPM for each ticker using the aggregated values.
# Handle potential division by zero if total_sell_amount_for_ticker is 0.
result = aggregated_by_ticker.CALCULATE(
    ticker_symbol=ticker_symbol,
    SPM=IFF(
        total_sell_amount_for_ticker == 0,
        0.0,  # SPM is 0 if there are no sales amounts to base it on
        (total_sell_amount_for_ticker - (total_tax_for_ticker + total_commission_for_ticker)) / total_sell_amount_for_ticker * 100.0
    )
)

# Define the start and end of the date range for "past month, inclusive of 1 month ago"
# This means from exactly one month ago up to the current moment.
date_from = DATETIME('now', '-1 month')
date_to = DATETIME('now')

# Filter Tickers to include only those that have "sell" transactions in the specified period.
# This ensures we only process and output tickers that had relevant sell activity.
tickers_with_relevant_sells = Tickers.WHERE(
    HAS(transactions_of.WHERE(
        (transaction_type == "sell") &
        (date_time >= date_from) &
        (date_time <= date_to)
    )) == 1
)

# For these tickers, calculate the required sums and then the SPM.
# The first CALCULATE computes the necessary aggregate sums.
# The second CALCULATE computes the SPM using these sums.
# The third CALCULATE ensures only the requested fields are in the final output.
result = tickers_with_relevant_sells.CALCULATE(
    # Store the ticker symbol from the Tickers collection
    ticker_symbol_val=symbol,

    # Calculate Total Amount from Sells for the period for the current ticker
    total_sell_amount_val=SUM(
        transactions_of.WHERE(
            (transaction_type == "sell") &
            (date_time >= date_from) &
            (date_time <= date_to)
        ).amount
    ),

    # Calculate Total (Tax + Commission) from Sells for the period for the current ticker
    # An inner CALCULATE is used for `tax + commission` before summing, as per PyDough rules.
    total_tax_and_commission_val=SUM(
        transactions_of.WHERE(
            (transaction_type == "sell") &
            (date_time >= date_from) &
            (date_time <= date_to)
        ).CALCULATE(
            tax_plus_comm=tax + commission  # Calculate sum of tax and commission per transaction
        ).tax_plus_comm  # Sum these per-transaction sums
    )
).CALCULATE(
    # Expose the ticker symbol with the final desired name
    ticker_symbol=ticker_symbol_val,

    # Calculate SPM using the previously calculated aggregate sums.
    # If total_sell_amount_val is 0, SPM is defined as 0.0 to avoid division by zero.
    spm=IFF(
        total_sell_amount_val == 0,
        0.0,
        (total_sell_amount_val - total_tax_and_commission_val) / total_sell_amount_val * 100.0
    )
).CALCULATE( # Final selection to ensure only the 'ticker_symbol' and 'spm' fields are output
    ticker_symbol=ticker_symbol,
    spm=spm
)

# Step 1: Augment DailyPrices with ticker symbol and formatted month string.
# LPAD is used to ensure the month is two digits (e.g., "01" for January).
daily_price_info_augmented = DailyPrices.CALCULATE(
    calc_ticker_symbol=ticker.symbol,
    calc_year_month_str=JOIN_STRINGS("-", YEAR(date), LPAD(MONTH(date), 2, "0"))
)

# Step 2: Create (ticker, month) groups.
# 'daily_prices_for_month_group' is the name for the collection of these groups.
# Each group contains daily price records for a specific ticker and month.
# The partition keys are calc_ticker_symbol and calc_year_month_str.
ticker_month_groups = daily_price_info_augmented.PARTITION(
    name="daily_prices_for_month_group",
    by=(calc_ticker_symbol, calc_year_month_str)
)

# Step 3: Group these (ticker, month) groups by ticker.
# 'ticker_level_group' is the name of this higher-level partition (by ticker symbol).
# This name will be used as the 'per' argument in the PREV function.
# The sub-collection under each 'ticker_level_group' will be the 'daily_prices_for_month_group' items
# that belong to that specific ticker.
ticker_data_grouped_by_symbol = ticker_month_groups.PARTITION(
    name="ticker_level_group",
    by=(calc_ticker_symbol)
)

# Step 4: Calculate monthly aggregates.
# This operation is performed on each (ticker, month) group within its respective ticker_level_group.
# 'calc_ticker_symbol' and 'calc_year_month_str' are available as they were partition keys.
# Aggregations (AVG, MAX, MIN) are applied to the original DailyPrices data scoped to the current group.
monthly_aggregates_per_ticker = ticker_data_grouped_by_symbol.ticker_level_group.daily_prices_for_month_group.CALCULATE(
    ticker_s_out=calc_ticker_symbol,
    month_ym_out=calc_year_month_str,
    avg_c_calc=AVG(DailyPrices.close),
    max_h_calc=MAX(DailyPrices.high),
    min_l_calc=MIN(DailyPrices.low)
)

# Step 5 & 6: Calculate PREV average close, MoMC, and select final fields.
# This first CALCULATE introduces prev_avg_close.
# The PREV function looks at 'avg_c_calc' from the previous record, ordered by 'month_ym_out',
# partitioned 'per="ticker_level_group"' (i.e., within each ticker).
# A default of None is provided for PREV if no prior record exists.
result = monthly_aggregates_per_ticker.CALCULATE(
    ticker_symbol=ticker_s_out,
    month=month_ym_out,
    average_closing_price=avg_c_calc,
    highest_price=max_h_calc,
    lowest_price=min_l_calc,
    prev_avg_close=PREV(avg_c_calc, by=month_ym_out.ASC(), per="ticker_level_group", default=None)
).CALCULATE( # This second CALCULATE computes MoMC using prev_avg_close.
    ticker_symbol=ticker_symbol,
    month=month,
    average_closing_price=average_closing_price,
    highest_price=highest_price,
    lowest_price=lowest_price,
    momc=IFF(
        prev_avg_close == None,  # Handle no previous month data
        None,
        IFF(
            prev_avg_close == 0,   # Handle division by zero
            None,
            (average_closing_price - prev_avg_close) / prev_avg_close
        )
    )
)

[DEBUG] [Q13] Code generated by gemini:
# Step 1: Prepare daily data by extracting necessary date parts and ticker symbol
# Also, rename fields to avoid conflicts and for clarity.
daily_price_details = DailyPrices.CALCULATE(
    calc_ticker_symbol=ticker.symbol,
    calc_year=YEAR(date),
    calc_month=MONTH(date),
    calc_close_price=close,
    calc_high_price=high,
    calc_low_price=low
)

# Step 2: Calculate monthly aggregates for each ticker
# This creates a collection where each row is a summary for a specific ticker-month.
monthly_aggregated_data = daily_price_details.PARTITION(
    name="group_by_ticker_and_month",  # Name for the partitioning operation
    by=(calc_ticker_symbol, calc_year, calc_month)
).CALCULATE(
    # Fields from the 'by' clause are available directly
    out_ticker_symbol=calc_ticker_symbol,
    out_year=calc_year,
    out_month_num=calc_month,
    # Aggregate calculations are performed on 'daily_price_details' within each group
    out_avg_close=AVG(daily_price_details.calc_close_price),
    out_max_high=MAX(daily_price_details.calc_high_price),
    out_min_low=MIN(daily_price_details.calc_low_price)
)

# Step 3: Calculate MoMC using the PREV window function
# Partition the monthly aggregates by ticker symbol to apply PREV per ticker.
# 'ticker_group_for_prev_calc' is the name of this ancestor partition for PREV's 'per' argument.
data_with_momc_calc = monthly_aggregated_data.PARTITION(
    name="ticker_group_for_prev_calc",
    by=(out_ticker_symbol)
).monthly_aggregated_data.CALCULATE(  # Access the sub-collection of monthly data for the current ticker
    # Carry forward necessary fields from the sub-collection 'monthly_aggregated_data'
    final_ticker_symbol=out_ticker_symbol, # This is also the partition key
    final_year_for_sort=out_year,
    final_month_for_sort=out_month_num,
    final_month_str=JOIN_STRINGS("-", out_year, LPAD(out_month_num, 2, "0")),
    final_avg_close=out_avg_close,
    final_max_high=out_max_high,
    final_min_low=out_min_low,

    # Calculate previous month's average closing price for the current ticker.
    # PREV operates on 'out_avg_close' from the sub-collection.
    # 'by' orders records chronologically within the current ticker's data.
    # 'per="ticker_group_for_prev_calc"' ensures PREV resets for each new ticker.
    # 'default=None' handles the first month for any ticker.
    prev_month_avg_close_val=PREV(
        out_avg_close,
        by=(out_year.ASC(), out_month_num.ASC()),
        per="ticker_group_for_prev_calc",
        default=None
    ),

    # Calculate MoMC. Division by None or zero (if prev_month_avg_close_val is 0) will result in None.
    calc_momc=(out_avg_close - PREV(out_avg_close, by=(out_year.ASC(), out_month_num.ASC()), per="ticker_group_for_prev_calc", default=None)) / PREV(out_avg_close, by=(out_year.ASC(), out_month_num.ASC()), per="ticker_group_for_prev_calc", default=None)
)

# Step 4: Select final fields and apply ordering
result = data_with_momc_calc.CALCULATE(
    ticker_symbol=final_ticker_symbol,
    month=final_month_str,
    average_closing_price=final_avg_close,
    highest_price=final_max_high,
    lowest_price=final_min_low,
    MoMC=calc_momc
).ORDER_BY(final_ticker_symbol.ASC(), data_with_momc_calc.final_year_for_sort.ASC(), data_with_momc_calc.final_month_for_sort.ASC())

# Step 1: Filter Tickers that have price data in the specified date range
# and calculate the overall min low and max high for their historical prices within that range.
price_data_for_tickers = Tickers.WHERE(
    HAS(historical_prices.WHERE((date >= "2023-04-01") & (date <= "2023-04-04"))) == 1
).CALCULATE(
    ticker_sym=symbol,
    overall_min_low=MIN(historical_prices.WHERE((date >= "2023-04-01") & (date <= "2023-04-04")).low),
    overall_max_high=MAX(historical_prices.WHERE((date >= "2023-04-01") & (date <= "2023-04-04")).high)
)

# Step 2: Calculate the price change for each of these tickers.
# Intermediate variable names are used to avoid conflicts with schema names.
tickers_with_price_change = price_data_for_tickers.CALCULATE(
    ticker_symbol_val=ticker_sym,
    price_change_val=(overall_max_high - overall_min_low)
)

# Step 3: Select the top 3 tickers based on the calculated price change, in descending order.
top_stocks_by_change = tickers_with_price_change.TOP_K(3, by=price_change_val.DESC())

# Step 4: Select the final output fields as requested: ticker_symbol and price_change.
result = top_stocks_by_change.CALCULATE(
    ticker_symbol=ticker_symbol_val,
    price_change=price_change_val
)

# Step 1: For each ticker, retrieve its symbol and filter its historical prices for the specified date range.
tickers_with_filtered_prices = Tickers.CALCULATE(
    ticker_symbol_calc=symbol,
    filtered_historical_prices=historical_prices.WHERE(
        (date >= "2023-04-01") & (date <= "2023-04-04")
    )
)

# Step 2: Filter out tickers that have no price data in the specified range.
# HAS(collection) checks if the collection contains at least one record.
tickers_having_prices_in_range = tickers_with_filtered_prices.WHERE(
    HAS(filtered_historical_prices) == 1
)

# Step 3: For each of these tickers, calculate the maximum high price, minimum low price,
# and the difference between them over the period.
ticker_price_stats = tickers_having_prices_in_range.CALCULATE(
    ticker_symbol_calc=ticker_symbol_calc,  # Carry forward the ticker symbol
    max_high_in_range=MAX(filtered_historical_prices.high),
    min_low_in_range=MIN(filtered_historical_prices.low)
).CALCULATE(
    ticker_symbol_calc=ticker_symbol_calc,  # Carry forward again
    price_difference=(max_high_in_range - min_low_in_range)
)

# Step 4: Select the top 3 tickers with the largest price difference.
# The results are ordered by price_difference in descending order.
top_3_stocks_by_change = ticker_price_stats.TOP_K(3, by=price_difference.DESC())

# Step 5: Format the final output to return the ticker symbol and the calculated price change.
result = top_3_stocks_by_change.CALCULATE(
    ticker_symbol=ticker_symbol_calc,
    price_change=price_difference
)

# Step 1: Filter DailyPrices for the specified date range.
# Also, retrieve the ticker symbol from the related Tickers table via the 'ticker' relationship,
# and select the high and low prices needed for calculation.
filtered_daily_prices = DailyPrices.WHERE(
    (date >= "2023-04-01") & (date <= "2023-04-04")
).CALCULATE(
    # Using 'dp_' prefix for clarity and to avoid potential name collisions with schema fields.
    dp_symbol=ticker.symbol,  # Accessing 'symbol' from the singular 'ticker' relationship.
    dp_high=high,             # Selecting the 'high' price for the day.
    dp_low=low                # Selecting the 'low' price for the day.
)

# Step 2: Partition the filtered data by stock symbol.
# For each distinct stock, this will allow us to calculate aggregate values over the period.
stock_data_aggregated = filtered_daily_prices.PARTITION(
    name="group_by_stock", by=(dp_symbol)  # Partitioning by the stock symbol.
).CALCULATE(
    # 'dp_symbol' is a partition key, so it can be directly used here for the output.
    output_stock_symbol=dp_symbol,
    # Aggregate functions (MAX, MIN) operate on the 'filtered_daily_prices' collection
    # within the context of the current partition group (i.e., for each distinct stock).
    # This finds the highest high and lowest low across all days in the range for the stock.
    period_max_high=MAX(filtered_daily_prices.dp_high),
    period_min_low=MIN(filtered_daily_prices.dp_low)
)

# Step 3: Calculate the price change for each stock and define final output field names.
# The price change is the difference between the maximum high and minimum low over the period.
stock_price_changes_calculated = stock_data_aggregated.CALCULATE(
    ticker_symbol=output_stock_symbol,  # Renaming for final output.
    price_change=period_max_high - period_min_low  # Calculating the difference.
)

# Step 4: Select the top 3 stocks with the largest price change.
# TOP_K sorts the records by 'price_change' in descending order and takes the first 3.
result = stock_price_changes_calculated.TOP_K(
    3, by=price_change.DESC()
)

    c_id=_id,

    c_join_year=YEAR(join_date)
)

    customer_identifier=c_id,

[DEBUG] [Q17] Code generated by gemini:
# Filter transactions to include only those within the period January 1, 2023, to March 31, 2023, inclusive.
# The DATETIME function is used to define the precise start and end of the period.
# date_time >= DATETIME("2023-01-01") ensures transactions from the start of Jan 1, 2023.
# date_time < DATETIME("2023-03-31", "+1 day") ensures transactions up to the end of Mar 31, 2023.
filtered_transactions = Transactions.WHERE(
    (date_time >= DATETIME("2023-01-01")) & (date_time < DATETIME("2023-03-31", "+1 day"))
)

# Group the filtered transactions by 'transaction_type'.
# For each transaction type, calculate:
# 1. 'num_distinct_customers': The count of unique customer_id's who made transactions of this type.
#    NDISTINCT(Transactions.customer_id) correctly counts distinct customers within each group.
# 2. 'avg_shares': The average number of shares for transactions of this type.
#    AVG(Transactions.shares) calculates this average within each group.
# The 'transaction_type' field itself is also included in the output of this step.
transaction_summary_by_type = filtered_transactions.PARTITION(
    name="groups_by_type", by=(transaction_type)
).CALCULATE(
    transaction_type=transaction_type,
    num_distinct_customers=NDISTINCT(Transactions.customer_id),
    avg_shares=AVG(Transactions.shares)
)

# From the summarized data, select the top 3 transaction types.
# The ranking is based on 'num_distinct_customers' in descending order.
# The result will contain 'transaction_type', 'num_distinct_customers', and 'avg_shares' for these top 3 types.
result = transaction_summary_by_type.TOP_K(
    3, by=num_distinct_customers.DESC()
)

# Step 1: For each transaction, get the customer's state and the ticker's type.
transaction_details = Transactions.CALCULATE(
    cust_state_val=customer.state,
    tick_type_val=ticker.ticker_type
)

# Step 2: Group transactions by the combination of customer state and ticker type.
# Step 3: For each group, count the number of transactions.
# The CALCULATE after PARTITION renames the partitioning keys for the output
# and computes the number of transactions for each combination.
combinations_by_transactions = transaction_details.PARTITION(
    name="state_ticker_groups", by=(cust_state_val, tick_type_val)
).CALCULATE(
    customer_state=cust_state_val,
    ticker_type=tick_type_val,
    num_transactions=COUNT(Transactions)  # Counts transactions within each group
)

# Step 4: Select the top 5 combinations by the number of transactions in descending order.
result = combinations_by_transactions.TOP_K(5, by=num_transactions.DESC())

[DEBUG] [Q26] Running models for: What are the top 5 countries by total transaction amount in the past 30 days, inclusive of 30 days ago? Return the country name, number of transactions and total transaction amount.
[DEBUG] [Q26] Running model claude (attempt 0)
[DEBUG] [Q26] Running model gemini (attempt 0)
[ERROR] [Q26] Model claude failed on attempt 0: [ClaudeAIProvider] Request failed: Streaming is strongly recommended for operations that may take longer than 10 minutes. See https://github.com/anthropics/anthropic-sdk-python#long-requests for more details
[DEBUG] [Q26] Code generated by gemini:
# Step 1: Filter transactions to include those from the last 30 days (inclusive of 30 days ago).
# DATEDIFF("days", date_time, DATETIME('now')) gives the number of full days between the transaction's date_time and now.
# <= 30 includes today (0 days diff) up to 30 full days ago.
recent_transactions_filtered = Transactions.WHERE(
    DATEDIFF("days", date_time, DATETIME('now')) <= 30
)

# Step 2: Add the customer's country to each transaction record.
# This makes 'customer_country_attr' available for partitioning.
# The 'amount' field is already part of Transactions and will be used for SUM.
transactions_with_country_info = recent_transactions_filtered.CALCULATE(
    customer_country_attr = customer.country
)

# Step 3 & 4: Group by country and calculate aggregates.
# - 'country_name' will be the grouping key (customer_country_attr).
# - 'number_of_transactions' is the count of transactions within each country group.
# - 'total_transaction_amount' is the sum of transaction amounts within each country group.
# Inside PARTITION's CALCULATE, COUNT(Transactions) and SUM(Transactions.amount) refer to
# the Transaction items belonging to each specific country group.
country_aggregates = transactions_with_country_info.PARTITION(
    name="country_group", by=(customer_country_attr)
).CALCULATE(
    country_name = customer_country_attr,
    number_of_transactions = COUNT(Transactions),
    total_transaction_amount = SUM(Transactions.amount)
)

# Step 5: Select the top 5 countries based on the total transaction amount.
top_countries_by_total_amount = country_aggregates.TOP_K(
    5, by=total_transaction_amount.DESC()
)

# Assign the final result to the 'result' variable.
result = top_countries_by_total_amount

[DEBUG] [Q26] Code generated by gemini:
# Step 1: Filter transactions to the last 30 days and prepare data for aggregation.
# We access the 'Transactions' collection.
# We filter transactions where the 'date_time' is within the last 30 days (inclusive).
# DATEDIFF("days", date_time, DATETIME('now')) calculates the number of full days
# between the transaction's date_time and the current moment.
# We select transactions where this difference is less than or equal to 30.
# For these filtered transactions, we then calculate 'cust_country' (the customer's country,
# accessed via the 'customer' relationship) and 'tx_amount' (the transaction amount).
# These fields are necessary for the subsequent grouping and aggregation.
transactions_in_period = Transactions.WHERE(
    DATEDIFF("days", date_time, DATETIME('now')) <= 30
).CALCULATE(
    cust_country=customer.country,
    tx_amount=amount
)

# Step 2: Group by country and calculate aggregates.
# We partition the 'transactions_in_period' collection by 'cust_country'.
# This creates groups of transactions for each unique customer country.
# For each country group (partition), we use CALCULATE to derive:
# - 'country_name': The name of the country, which is the 'cust_country' itself.
# - 'num_transactions': The total number of transactions for that country within the specified period.
#   COUNT(transactions_in_period) counts the records from 'transactions_in_period' that fall into the current partition group.
# - 'total_transaction_amount': The sum of all transaction amounts for that country within the period.
#   SUM(transactions_in_period.tx_amount) sums the 'tx_amount' field for records in the current partition group.
country_aggregates = transactions_in_period.PARTITION(name="country_group", by=(cust_country)).CALCULATE(
    country_name=cust_country,
    num_transactions=COUNT(transactions_in_period),
    total_transaction_amount=SUM(transactions_in_period.tx_amount)
)

# Step 3: Select the top 5 countries by total transaction amount.
# We use the TOP_K function on the 'country_aggregates' collection.
# This selects the top 5 records, ordered by 'total_transaction_amount' in descending order (highest amount first).
# The resulting collection will contain the 'country_name', 'num_transactions',
# and 'total_transaction_amount' for these top 5 countries.
result = country_aggregates.TOP_K(5, by=total_transaction_amount.DESC())

# Step 1: Filter transactions for the last 30 days and get the customer's country.
# DATEDIFF("days", date_time, DATETIME('now')) <= 30 ensures we include transactions
# from today up to 30 days ago.
# We also calculate 'c_country' from the related customer for partitioning.
transactions_with_country_info = Transactions.WHERE(
    DATEDIFF("days", date_time, DATETIME('now')) <= 30
).CALCULATE(
    c_country=customer.country
)

# Step 2: Partition the filtered transactions by the customer's country.
# Then, for each country group, calculate the country name,
# the total number of transactions, and the sum of transaction amounts.
country_level_summary = transactions_with_country_info.PARTITION(
    name="country_group", by=(c_country)  # Partitioning by the calculated c_country
).CALCULATE(
    country_name=c_country,  # The country name is the partition key
    num_transactions=COUNT(Transactions),  # Count of transactions in this country group
    total_transaction_amount=SUM(Transactions.amount)  # Sum of transaction amounts for this country group
)

# Step 3: Select the top 5 countries based on the total_transaction_amount in descending order.
top_5_countries_by_total_amount = country_level_summary.TOP_K(
    5, by=total_transaction_amount.DESC()
)

result = top_5_countries_by_total_amount

# Step 1: For each customer, calculate the number of "sell" transactions on 2023-04-01.
# Also, select the customer's original _id and name, aliasing them to avoid conflicts.
customer_transaction_counts = Customers.CALCULATE(
    customer_identifier=_id,
    customer_name_val=name,
    num_sell_transactions_on_date=COUNT(
        transactions_made.WHERE(
            (transaction_type == "sell") &
            (YEAR(date_time) == 2023) &
            (MONTH(date_time) == 4) &
            (DAY(date_time) == 1)
        )
    )
)

# Step 2: From the above, select the top customer based on the number of sell transactions.
# TOP_K(1, ...) will return the single customer with the most transactions.
# If there's a tie, one of the top customers will be selected.
top_customer_by_sales = customer_transaction_counts.TOP_K(
    1,
    by=num_sell_transactions_on_date.DESC()
)

# Step 3: Format the final output to include _id, name, and the number of transactions.
# The field names in the CALCULATE clause match the requested output.
result = top_customer_by_sales.CALCULATE(
    _id=customer_identifier,
    name=customer_name_val,
    number_of_transactions=num_sell_transactions_on_date
)

customer_sell_counts = Customers.CALCULATE(
    # Store original _id and name for later use, using intermediate names
    # to avoid collision if _id or name were part of the calculation logic itself.
    # Here, it's mainly for clarity before the final CALCULATE.
    original_customer_id=_id,
    customer_name=name,
    num_specific_sell_transactions=COUNT(
        transactions_made.WHERE(
            (transaction_type == "sell") &
            (DATETIME(date_time, 'start of day') == "2023-04-01")
        )
    )
)

    1,
    by=num_specific_sell_transactions.DESC()
)

# Select the required fields: _id, name, and the number of transactions
result = top_customer_by_sell_transactions.CALCULATE(
    _id=original_customer_id,
    name=customer_name,
    num_transactions=num_specific_sell_transactions

        )
    )
).TOP_K(

).CALCULATE(

    _id=customer_id_val,
    name=customer_name_val,
    number_of_transactions=num_sell_transactions_on_date

# Step 1: Filter transactions for Q1 2023, successful status, and extract the month number.
# We assume 'successful' is the string value in the 'status' field for successful transactions.
filtered_transactions_with_month = Transactions.WHERE(
    (YEAR(date_time) == 2023) &
    (MONTH(date_time) >= 1) & # January
    (MONTH(date_time) <= 3) & # March
    (status == "successful")
).CALCULATE(
    transaction_month_value = MONTH(date_time)
)

# Step 2: Partition by the extracted month and calculate the average transaction price for each month.
# The AVG(Transactions.price) will calculate the average over the 'price' attribute
# of the transactions belonging to each monthly group.
monthly_avg_transaction_price = filtered_transactions_with_month.PARTITION(
    name="monthly_transaction_groups", by=(transaction_month_value)
).CALCULATE(
    month_number = transaction_month_value,
    avg_transaction_price = AVG(Transactions.price)
).ORDER_BY(month_number.ASC())

# Filter transactions for Q1 2023 and successful status
q1_successful_transactions = Transactions.WHERE(
    (YEAR(date_time) == 2023) &
    ISIN(MONTH(date_time), (1, 2, 3)) &
    (status == "successful")
)

    transaction_month = MONTH(date_time)
)

# Partition by transaction month

    name="monthly_data",
    by=(transaction_month)
)

# Calculate the average transaction price for each month
result = monthly_groups.CALCULATE(
    month = transaction_month,
    avg_transaction_price = AVG(Transactions.price)
).ORDER_BY(month.ASC())

[DEBUG] [Q32] Running models for: For sales with sale price over $30,000, how many payments were received in total and on weekends in each of the last 8 calendar weeks (excluding the current week)? Return the week (as a date), total payments received, and weekend payments received in ascending order.
[DEBUG] [Q32] Running model claude (attempt 0)
[DEBUG] [Q32] Running model gemini (attempt 0)
[ERROR] [Q32] Model claude failed on attempt 0: [ClaudeAIProvider] Request failed: Streaming is strongly recommended for operations that may take longer than 10 minutes. See https://github.com/anthropics/anthropic-sdk-python#long-requests for more details
[DEBUG] [Q32] Code generated by gemini:
# Define the start of the current week and the start of the 8-week period prior to it.
# The period is [eight_weeks_ago_start, current_week_start_date_boundary)
current_week_start_date_boundary = DATETIME('now', 'start of week')
eight_weeks_ago_start_date = DATETIME(current_week_start_date_boundary, '-8 weeks')

# 1. Filter Sales for those with sale_price > $30,000
high_value_sales = Dealership.Sales.WHERE(sale_price > 30000)

# 2. Access PaymentsReceived associated with these sales
# 'payment' is the sub-collection of PaymentsReceived for a Sale
payments_for_high_value_sales = high_value_sales.payment

# 3. Filter these payments to include only those within the last 8 full weeks (excluding current week)
relevant_payments_in_window = payments_for_high_value_sales.WHERE(
    (payment_date >= eight_weeks_ago_start_date) & (payment_date < current_week_start_date_boundary)
)

# 4. For each payment, determine its week's start date and if it was made on a weekend
payments_with_week_details = relevant_payments_in_window.CALCULATE(
    # Calculate the start date of the week for each payment
    payment_week_start_calc = DATETIME(payment_date, 'start of week'),
    # Check if the payment day name is Saturday or Sunday
    is_weekend_payment_calc = ISIN(UPPER(DAYNAME(payment_date)), ("SATURDAY", "SUNDAY"))
)

# 5. Group payments by their calculated week_start_date and aggregate counts
weekly_payment_summary = payments_with_week_details.PARTITION(
    name="payments_by_week", by=(payment_week_start_calc)
).CALCULATE(
    week_date = payment_week_start_calc,  # The week's start date (partition key)
    total_payments_received = COUNT(payments_with_week_details),  # Total payments in this week
    weekend_payments_received = SUM(IFF(payments_with_week_details.is_weekend_payment_calc, 1, 0))  # Sum of weekend payments
)

# 6. Order the results by week date in ascending order
final_result = weekly_payment_summary.ORDER_BY(week_date.ASC())

toyota_sales_last_30_days = Sales.WHERE(
    (Sales.car.make == "Toyota") &
    (DATEDIFF("days", Sales.sale_date, DATETIME('now')) >= 0) & # Ensures sale_date is not in the future
    (DATEDIFF("days", Sales.sale_date, DATETIME('now')) <= 29)  # Includes sales from today up to 29 days ago
)

# Step 1: Calculate total sales and number of sales for each salesperson.
# We use temporary names for the calculated fields (e.g., temp_total_sales)
# to clearly distinguish them before they are mapped to the final output names.
sales_aggregates = Salespersons.CALCULATE(
    salesperson_first_name=first_name,
    salesperson_last_name=last_name,
    temp_total_sales=SUM(sales_made.sale_price),
    temp_num_sales=COUNT(sales_made)
)

# Step 2: Calculate the sales rank (SR) based on the temporary total sales,
# map fields to their final requested names, and order the results.
# The RANKING function is applied to the collection generated by 'sales_aggregates'.
# SR is calculated by ranking salespersons based on 'temp_total_sales' in descending order.
# The final result is ordered by SR in ascending order, so the salesperson with rank 1 (highest sales) appears first.
result = sales_aggregates.CALCULATE(
    first_name=salesperson_first_name,
    last_name=salesperson_last_name,
    total_sales_amount=temp_total_sales,
    number_of_sales=temp_num_sales,
    SR=RANKING(by=temp_total_sales.DESC())
).ORDER_BY(SR.ASC())

[DEBUG] [Q37] Code generated by gemini:
# Step 1: Calculate properties for each car: if it's sold and its latest inventory status.
cars_with_status_info = Cars.CALCULATE(
    # Keep original car properties needed for partitioning and accessing sales
    car_make=make,
    car_model=model,
    # Flag indicating if the car has at least one sale record
    is_sold_flag=(HAS(sale_records) == 1),
    # Flag indicating if the car's latest inventory snapshot shows it's not in inventory.
    # RANKING is used to find the latest snapshot for each car (per="Cars").
    # SINGULAR() picks one if there are ties in snapshot_date for the latest.
    # We then check if its 'is_in_inventory' status is False.
    latest_snapshot_is_not_in_inventory_flag=(
        inventory_snapshots.WHERE(
            RANKING(by=snapshot_date.DESC(), per="Cars", allow_ties=True) == 1
        ).SINGULAR().is_in_inventory == False
    )
)

# Step 2: Filter for cars that are sold AND whose latest inventory status is 'not in inventory'.
relevant_cars_for_report = cars_with_status_info.WHERE(
    is_sold_flag & latest_snapshot_is_not_in_inventory_flag
)

# Step 3: Partition the relevant cars by make and model.
# For each group, calculate the highest sale price from their sale records.
make_model_max_sales = relevant_cars_for_report.PARTITION(
    name="make_model_group", by=(car_make, car_model)
).CALCULATE(
    final_car_make=car_make,  # Renaming for clarity in the final output
    final_car_model=car_model, # Renaming for clarity in the final output
    # MAX(Cars.sale_records.sale_price) gets the maximum sale_price from all sale_records
    # of all cars belonging to the current make/model partition group.
    highest_sale_price_val=MAX(Cars.sale_records.sale_price)
)

# Step 4: Order the results by the calculated highest sale price in descending order.
result = make_model_max_sales.ORDER_BY(highest_sale_price_val.DESC())

[DEBUG] [Q37] Code generated by gemini:
# Step 1: For each car, calculate its make, model, whether it has sales, and its latest inventory status.
# 'latest_inventory_status' is derived from the most recent snapshot for that car.
# RANKING(by=snapshot_date.DESC(), per="Cars", allow_ties=False) == 1 identifies the latest snapshot.
# SINGULAR() is used as we expect one latest status per car.
cars_with_status_info = Cars.CALCULATE(
    car_make_field=make,
    car_model_field=model,
    has_sales_records=HAS(sale_records) == 1,
    latest_inventory_status=inventory_snapshots.WHERE(
        RANKING(by=snapshot_date.DESC(), per="Cars", allow_ties=False) == 1
    ).SINGULAR().is_in_inventory
)

# Step 2: Filter these cars to include only those that have been sold and are no longer in inventory.
filtered_cars_for_analysis = cars_with_status_info.WHERE(
    (has_sales_records == True) & (latest_inventory_status == False)
)

# Step 3: Partition the filtered cars by make and model.
# Then, for each make/model group, calculate the highest sale price.
# 'Cars.sale_records.sale_price' refers to the sale prices of cars within the current partition group.
make_model_max_sales_price = filtered_cars_for_analysis.PARTITION(
    name="make_model_group", by=(car_make_field, car_model_field)
).CALCULATE(
    make=car_make_field,  # Output field 'make'
    model=car_model_field, # Output field 'model'
    highest_sale_price=MAX(Cars.sale_records.sale_price)
)

# Step 4: Order the results by the highest sale price in descending order.
result = make_model_max_sales_price.ORDER_BY(highest_sale_price.DESC())

# Define the start and end of the target period: last 6 months, excluding the current month.
# For example, if today is 2024-07-15:
# current_month_start would be 2024-07-01.
# six_months_ago_start would be 2024-07-01 minus 6 months = 2024-01-01.
# The period is [2024-01-01, 2024-07-01).
six_months_ago_start_date = DATETIME('now', 'start of month', '-6 months')
current_month_start_date = DATETIME('now', 'start of month')

# Filter sales based on salesperson hire year and sale date period
relevant_sales = Sales.WHERE(
    (YEAR(salesperson.hire_date) >= 2022) &  # Salesperson hired in 2022 or later
    (YEAR(salesperson.hire_date) <= 2023) &  # Salesperson hired in 2023 or earlier
    (sale_date >= six_months_ago_start_date) &    # Sale date is within the last 6 months (start)
    (sale_date < current_month_start_date)       # Sale date is before the current month (end)
)

# Prepare sales data by creating a month representation for grouping
sales_prepared_for_grouping = relevant_sales.CALCULATE(
    # Create a "YYYY-MM" string representation for the sale month
    sale_month_year_text = JOIN_STRINGS("-", YEAR(sale_date), LPAD(MONTH(sale_date), 2, "0"))
    # sale_price is implicitly carried over or accessible from the original Sales collection in PARTITION
)

# Group by the calculated month and aggregate to get PMSPS and PMSR
monthly_sales_metrics = sales_prepared_for_grouping.PARTITION(name="sales_per_month", by=(sale_month_year_text)).CALCULATE(
    month = sale_month_year_text,  # The month identifier (YYYY-MM)
    PMSPS = COUNT(Sales),          # Per Month Salesperson Sales count
    PMSR = SUM(Sales.sale_price)   # Per Month Sales Revenue
)

# Order the results by month in ascending order
final_monthly_report = monthly_sales_metrics.ORDER_BY(month.ASC())

# Define date boundaries for the last 6 months, excluding the current month.
# For example, if 'now' is 2024-07-15:
# var_current_month_start will be 2024-07-01.
# var_period_start_date will be 2024-01-01 (DATETIME('2024-07-01', '-6 months')).
# Sales will be included if their date is from 2024-01-01 up to (but not including) 2024-07-01.
var_current_month_start = DATETIME('now', 'start of month')
var_period_start_date = DATETIME(var_current_month_start, '-6 months')

# 1. Create a collection of all sales, calculating their sale month in 'YYYY-MM' format.
all_sales_with_month_info = Sales.CALCULATE(
    calc_sale_month_year = JOIN_STRINGS("-", YEAR(sale_date), LPAD(MONTH(sale_date), 2, "0")),
    calc_sale_date = sale_date # Keep the original sale_date for filtering the period
)

# 2. Partition these sales by month_year to get unique months.
#    Then, filter these unique months to ensure they fall within the last 6 full months.
#    This gives us a base list of months that had at least one sale in the dealership and are in the target period.
active_months_in_period = all_sales_with_month_info.PARTITION(name="all_sales_by_month_group", by=(calc_sale_month_year)).CALCULATE(
    month_label = calc_sale_month_year,
    # Use MIN(Sales.calc_sale_date) to get a representative date for the month group to filter by the period.
    # This ensures the month itself falls within the 6-month window.
    min_sale_date_for_month = MIN(Sales.calc_sale_date)
).WHERE(
    (min_sale_date_for_month >= var_period_start_date) & (min_sale_date_for_month < var_current_month_start)
)

# 3. For each of these active months, calculate PMSPS and PMSR.
#    The calculations will consider only sales from salespersons hired between 2022-2023
#    and whose sales occurred in the respective 'month_label'.
#    If a 'month_label' had no sales from this specific group of salespersons, COUNT and SUM will yield 0.
final_monthly_metrics = active_months_in_period.CALCULATE(
    month = month_label,  # The 'YYYY-MM' month string
    PMSPS = COUNT(Sales.WHERE(
        (YEAR(salesperson.hire_date) >= 2022) & 
        (YEAR(salesperson.hire_date) <= 2023) &
        # Ensure sales are matched to the current 'month_label' from the outer CALCULATE
        (JOIN_STRINGS("-", YEAR(sale_date), LPAD(MONTH(sale_date), 2, "0")) == month_label)
    )),
    PMSR = SUM(Sales.WHERE(
        (YEAR(salesperson.hire_date) >= 2022) & 
        (YEAR(salesperson.hire_date) <= 2023) &
        # Ensure sales are matched to the current 'month_label'
        (JOIN_STRINGS("-", YEAR(sale_date), LPAD(MONTH(sale_date), 2, "0")) == month_label)
    ).sale_price)
).ORDER_BY(month.ASC())

# Assign the final result to the specified variable name.
pmsps_pmsr_last_6_months = final_monthly_metrics

# Step 1: Filter sales to find those where the car was sold on the same day it was marked as out of inventory.
# The inner WHERE clause checks the car's inventory snapshots:
# - is_in_inventory == False: The car is no longer in inventory.
# - snapshot_date == sale_date: The date it went out of inventory matches the sale date.
# HAS(...) ensures such an inventory snapshot exists for the car associated with the sale.

    HAS(car.inventory_snapshots.WHERE((is_in_inventory == False) & (snapshot_date == sale_date))) == 1
)

# TOP_K(1, by=sale_price.DESC()) returns a collection containing the single highest-priced sale.

# car.make and car.model refer to the attributes of the car associated with this sale.
result = highest_priced_sale.CALCULATE(
    make=car.make,
    model=car.model,
    sale_price=sale_price
)

[DEBUG] [Q44] Running models for: What is the total payments received per month? Also calculate the MoM change for each month.MoM change = (current month value - prev month value). Return all months in your answer, including those where there were no payments.
[DEBUG] [Q44] Running model claude (attempt 0)
[DEBUG] [Q44] Running model gemini (attempt 0)
[ERROR] [Q44] Model claude failed on attempt 0: [ClaudeAIProvider] Request failed: Streaming is strongly recommended for operations that may take longer than 10 minutes. See https://github.com/anthropics/anthropic-sdk-python#long-requests for more details
[DEBUG] [Q44] Code generated by gemini:
# Step 1: Access PaymentsReceived and extract year and month from payment_date.
# No need to calculate p_amount here as SUM will access the original collection field.
payments_info = Dealership.PaymentsReceived.CALCULATE(
    p_year=YEAR(payment_date),
    p_month=MONTH(payment_date)
)

# Step 2: Group by year and month to get total payments for each month.
# The 'name' in PARTITION defines how to refer to the group.
# SUM(Dealership.PaymentsReceived.payment_amount) sums the original payment_amount
# for records falling into each (p_year, p_month) group.
# DEFAULT_TO ensures that if SUM results in NULL (e.g., for an empty group or all NULL amounts),
# it's treated as 0.0.
monthly_totals = payments_info.PARTITION(
    name="month_group", by=(p_year, p_month)
).CALCULATE(
    year_val=p_year,
    month_val=p_month,
    total_monthly_payments=DEFAULT_TO(SUM(Dealership.PaymentsReceived.payment_amount), 0.0)
)

# Step 3: Calculate MoM change on the monthly_totals.
# The PREV function looks at the previous record based on the ordering specified in its 'by' clause.
# 'default=0.0' is used for the PREV function if there's no preceding record (e.g., for the first month in the series).
result = monthly_totals.CALCULATE(
    year_val=year_val,
    month_val=month_val,
    total_payments_for_month=total_monthly_payments,
    mom_change=total_monthly_payments - PREV(total_monthly_payments, by=(year_val.ASC(), month_val.ASC()), default=0.0)
).ORDER_BY(year_val.ASC(), month_val.ASC())

# Step 1: Access PaymentsReceived and extract year and month from payment_date.
# No need to calculate p_amount here as SUM will access the original collection field.
payments_info = Dealership.PaymentsReceived.CALCULATE(
    p_year=YEAR(payment_date),
    p_month=MONTH(payment_date)
)

# Step 2: Group by year and month to get total payments for each month.
# The 'name' in PARTITION defines how to refer to the group.
# SUM(Dealership.PaymentsReceived.payment_amount) sums the original payment_amount
# for records falling into each (p_year, p_month) group.
# DEFAULT_TO ensures that if SUM results in NULL (e.g., for an empty group or all NULL amounts),
# it's treated as 0.0.
monthly_totals = payments_info.PARTITION(
    name="month_group", by=(p_year, p_month)
).CALCULATE(
    year_val=p_year,
    month_val=p_month,
    total_monthly_payments=DEFAULT_TO(SUM(Dealership.PaymentsReceived.payment_amount), 0.0)
)

# Step 3: Calculate MoM change on the monthly_totals.
# The PREV function looks at the previous record based on the ordering specified in its 'by' clause.
# 'default=0.0' is used for the PREV function if there's no preceding record (e.g., for the first month in the series).
result = monthly_totals.CALCULATE(
    year_val=year_val,
    month_val=month_val,
    total_payments_for_month=total_monthly_payments,
    mom_change=total_monthly_payments - PREV(total_monthly_payments, by=(year_val.ASC(), month_val.ASC()), default=0.0)
).ORDER_BY(year_val.ASC(), month_val.ASC())

# Also, order by year and month to prepare for the PREV function.
monthly_aggregated_payments = PaymentsReceived.CALCULATE(
    pay_year=YEAR(payment_date),
    pay_month=MONTH(payment_date)
).PARTITION(
    name="monthly_group", by=(pay_year, pay_month)
).CALCULATE(
    payment_year_val=pay_year,
    payment_month_val=pay_month,
    total_payments_for_month_val=SUM(PaymentsReceived.payment_amount)
).ORDER_BY(payment_year_val.ASC(), payment_month_val.ASC())

# Step 2: Calculate the MoM change using the PREV window function.
# PREV will get the total_payments_for_month_val from the previous row (previous month).
result = monthly_aggregated_payments.CALCULATE(
    payment_year=payment_year_val,
    payment_month=payment_month_val,
    total_payments=total_payments_for_month_val,
    mom_change=(
        total_payments_for_month_val -
        PREV(total_payments_for_month_val, by=(payment_year_val.ASC(), payment_month_val.ASC()), default=0.0)
    )

# Step 1: Partition Cars by make and model to aggregate sales data for each model.
# For each group (make, model), calculate total revenue and total sales count.
car_model_aggregates = Dealership.Cars.PARTITION(name="car_model_groups", by=(make, model)).CALCULATE(
    # Store the make and model from the partition keys.
    # Using distinct names for these calculated fields to avoid collision with original field names.
    car_make_key=make,
    car_model_key=model,
    # Calculate total revenue by summing the 'sale_price' from all 'sale_records'
    # associated with the cars in the current (make, model) group.
    # 'Cars.sale_records' here refers to the sale_records of the cars belonging to the current partition group.
    calculated_total_revenue=SUM(Cars.sale_records.sale_price),
    # Calculate total number of sales by counting all 'sale_records'
    # associated with the cars in the current (make, model) group.
    calculated_total_sales=COUNT(Cars.sale_records)
)

# Step 2: Select the top 5 car models based on the calculated total revenue in descending order.
top_revenue_models = car_model_aggregates.TOP_K(5, by=calculated_total_revenue.DESC())

# Step 3: Format the output to include the make, model, total number of sales, and total revenue
# as requested in the query.
result = top_revenue_models.CALCULATE(
    make=car_make_key,
    model=car_model_key,
    total_number_of_sales=calculated_total_sales,
    total_revenue=calculated_total_revenue
)

# Access Salespersons collection from the Dealership graph
sales_representatives = Dealership.Salespersons

# Calculate total revenue and number of sales for each salesperson within the last 3 months.
# Intermediate field names (e.g., rep_first_name, total_revenue_amount) are used to store values
# before mapping them to the final requested output field names.
sales_performance = sales_representatives.CALCULATE(
    # Store original first_name and last_name for later output
    rep_first_name=first_name,
    rep_last_name=last_name,
    # Calculate total revenue from sales made in the last 3 months (inclusive of today)
    total_revenue_amount=SUM(
        sales_made.WHERE(
            (sale_date >= DATETIME('now', '-3 months')) & (sale_date <= DATETIME('now'))
        ).sale_price
    ),
    # Calculate total number of sales made in the last 3 months (inclusive of today)
    total_sales_count=COUNT(
        sales_made.WHERE(
            (sale_date >= DATETIME('now', '-3 months')) & (sale_date <= DATETIME('now'))
        )
    )
)

# Select the top 3 salespersons by their total revenue amount in descending order.
top_performers = sales_performance.TOP_K(3, by=total_revenue_amount.DESC())

# Format the output to return the requested fields: first_name, last_name, total_sales, and total_revenue.
result = top_performers.CALCULATE(
    first_name=rep_first_name,
    last_name=rep_last_name,

)

    (vendor_name == "Utility Company") &

).CALCULATE(

)

# If the output needs to be just the number, and the previous query returns a collection with one row and one column:
# We can use the high-level collection to calculate the count directly.

    count_payments=COUNT(
        PaymentsMade.WHERE(

# Calculate the start and end dates for the previous ISO week
# 'start of week' truncates to the beginning of the current ISO week (e.g., Monday)
# Then we subtract days to get to the previous week's boundaries.
start_of_current_week = DATETIME('now', 'start of week')
start_of_previous_week = DATETIME(start_of_current_week, '-7 days')
end_of_previous_week = DATETIME(start_of_current_week, '-1 day')

# Filter payments received within the previous ISO week
payments_in_previous_week = PaymentsReceived.WHERE(
    (payment_date >= start_of_previous_week) &
    (payment_date <= end_of_previous_week)
)

# Group by payment_date and payment_method, then calculate the sum of payment_amount
daily_payments_by_method = payments_in_previous_week.PARTITION(
    name="daily_method_group", by=(payment_date, payment_method)
).CALCULATE(
    payment_day=payment_date,
    method=payment_method,
    total_daily_amount=SUM(PaymentsReceived.payment_amount)
).ORDER_BY(payment_day.ASC(), method.ASC())

result = daily_payments_by_method

quarterly_sales_report = Sales.WHERE(
    YEAR(sale_date) == 2023  # Filter sales for the year 2023
).CALCULATE(
    # Extract customer's state for grouping
    cust_state_val=customer.state,
    # Determine the number of months to add to the start of the year to get the quarter's first date
    # Q1: 0 months (Jan), Q2: 3 months (Apr), Q3: 6 months (Jul), Q4: 9 months (Oct)
    months_to_add_for_quarter_start_str=IFF(
        MONTH(sale_date) <= 3, "0",  # Months 1-3 are Q1
        IFF(MONTH(sale_date) <= 6, "3",  # Months 4-6 are Q2
            IFF(MONTH(sale_date) <= 9, "6",  # Months 7-9 are Q3
                "9"  # Months 10-12 are Q4
            )
        )
    ),
    # Construct the date representing the first day of the quarter
    quarter_representation_date=DATETIME(
        DATETIME(sale_date, 'start of year'),  # Gets the first day of the year (e.g., 2023-01-01)
        JOIN_STRINGS(" ", months_to_add_for_quarter_start_str, "months")  # Adds 0, 3, 6, or 9 months
    )
    # Note: sale_price is implicitly carried forward for aggregation if not renamed or excluded.
    # To be explicit for SUM, we'll refer to Sales.sale_price in the aggregation step.
).PARTITION(
    name="sales_by_state_and_quarter",  # Name for the partition group
    by=(cust_state_val, quarter_representation_date)  # Group by customer state and the quarter date
).CALCULATE(
    customer_state=cust_state_val,  # Select the customer state
    quarter_first_date=quarter_representation_date,  # Select the quarter representation date
    total_quarterly_sales=SUM(Sales.sale_price)  # Sum sales prices for each group
)

# Filter sales for the year 2023 and prepare data for partitioning
sales_prepared_for_partition = Sales.WHERE(YEAR(sale_date) == 2023).CALCULATE(
    cust_state_attr=customer.state,  # Get the customer's state
    # sale_price will be accessed directly in SUM during aggregation
    _sale_month=MONTH(sale_date),  # Temporary variable to determine the quarter
    _year_start=DATETIME(sale_date, 'start of year'),  # Gets '2023-01-01' for all sales in 2023
    # Calculate the first date of the quarter
    quarter_start_dt=IFF(
        _sale_month <= 3,  # For Q1 (Jan-Mar)
        _year_start,  # Results in '2023-01-01'
        IFF(
            _sale_month <= 6,  # For Q2 (Apr-Jun)
            DATETIME(_year_start, '+3 months'),  # Results in '2023-04-01'
            IFF(
                _sale_month <= 9,  # For Q3 (Jul-Sep)
                DATETIME(_year_start, '+6 months'),  # Results in '2023-07-01'
                # For Q4 (Oct-Dec)
                DATETIME(_year_start, '+9 months')  # Results in '2023-10-01'
            )
        )
    )
)

# Group by customer state and the calculated quarter start date, then sum the sales for each group
total_quarterly_sales_by_state = sales_prepared_for_partition.PARTITION(
    name="sales_by_state_and_quarter",  # Name for the partition group
    by=(cust_state_attr, quarter_start_dt)  # Partition keys
).CALCULATE(
    customer_state=cust_state_attr,  # Output field for customer state
    quarter_date=quarter_start_dt,  # Output field for the first date of the quarter
    total_sales=SUM(Sales.sale_price)  # Calculate total sales for the group
)

# Filter sales for 2023 and prepare fields for grouping
sales_prepared_for_grouping = Sales.WHERE(YEAR(sale_date) == 2023).CALCULATE(
    # Calculate the first day of the quarter for the sale_date.
    # The logic is: take the sale_date, go to the start of its month,
    # then subtract months to get to the first month of the quarter.
    # (MONTH(sale_date)-1)%3 gives 0 for 1st month of quarter (e.g., Jan, Apr),
    # 1 for 2nd month (e.g., Feb, May), 2 for 3rd month (e.g., Mar, Jun).
    # So, we subtract that many months.
    first_day_of_quarter=DATETIME(sale_date, 'start of month', JOIN_STRINGS('', '-', STRING((MONTH(sale_date)-1)%3), ' months')),
    customer_s_state=customer.state
    # sale_price is implicitly available from the Sales collection for SUM
)

# Partition by the customer's state and the calculated first day of the quarter,
# then calculate the total sales for each group.
quarterly_sales_by_state_report = sales_prepared_for_grouping.PARTITION(
    name="state_quarter_groups", # Name for the partition group
    by=(customer_s_state, first_day_of_quarter) # Group by these calculated fields
).CALCULATE(
    state=customer_s_state, # Output field for customer's state
    quarter=first_day_of_quarter, # Output field for the first date of the quarter
    total_sales=SUM(Sales.sale_price) # Sum of sale_price for sales in this group
)

# Step 1: Prepare treatment data with month information and biologic flag
treatment_info = Treatments.CALCULATE(
    # Create a YYYY-MM string for the treatment month for partitioning and display
    treatment_year_month_str = JOIN_STRINGS("-", YEAR(start_dt), LPAD(MONTH(start_dt), 2, "0")),
    # Create a date representing the start of the treatment month for filtering
    treatment_month_start_date = DATETIME(start_dt, 'start of month'),
    patient_key = patient_id,  # Use an alias for patient_id
    is_biologic_treatment = (drug.drug_type == 'biologic') # Check if the drug used is biologic
)

# Step 2: Define date boundaries and filter for the last 3 full months
# Calculate the start of the current month
current_month_start_boundary = DATETIME('now', 'start of month')
# Calculate the start of the month that was 3 months prior to the current month
three_months_prior_start_boundary = DATETIME('now', 'start of month', '-3 months')

# Filter treatments to include only those within the defined date range
recent_treatment_info = treatment_info.WHERE(
    (treatment_month_start_date >= three_months_prior_start_boundary) &
    (treatment_month_start_date < current_month_start_boundary)
)

# Step 3: Partition by month and calculate distinct patient counts
result = recent_treatment_info.PARTITION(
    name="monthly_data", by=(treatment_year_month_str)  # Group data by the YYYY-MM string
).CALCULATE(
    month = treatment_year_month_str,  # Output the month string
    # Count distinct patients who had any treatment in this month
    patient_count = NDISTINCT(patient_key),

).ORDER_BY(month.ASC()) # Step 4: Order results by month

# Filter treatments for the relevant period and prepare for partitioning
# by creating a year-month string.

    (start_dt >= three_months_ago_start_date) & (start_dt < current_month_start_date)
).CALCULATE(
    # Create a sortable and groupable year-month string, e.g., "2023-09"
    treatment_year_month_str = JOIN_STRINGS("-", YEAR(start_dt), LPAD(MONTH(start_dt), 2, "0"))
)

# Partition the filtered treatments by the year-month string
# Each partition (monthly_data) will contain treatments for a specific month

    name="monthly_data",  # Name for the partition instance
    by=(treatment_year_month_str)
)

    biologic_distinct_patients = NDISTINCT(
        monthly_data.Treatments.WHERE(drug.drug_type == 'biologic').patient.patient_id
    )

    # Alias patient_id to avoid potential naming conflicts and for clarity

    # Flag to identify if the treatment used a biologic drug

    month = treatment_month_display_label,
    patient_count = NDISTINCT(patient_key),
    biologic_treatment_count = NDISTINCT(KEEP_IF(patient_key, is_biologic_drug_treatment == True))
)

# Define date expressions for the 12-month period, excluding the current month
current_month_start_expr = DATETIME('now', 'start of month')
twelve_months_ago_start_expr = DATETIME(current_month_start_expr, '-12 months')

# Filter treatments to the specified period and calculate the month of treatment.
# Explicitly include patient_id for the PMPD calculation.
treatments_in_period = Treatments.WHERE(
    (start_dt >= twelve_months_ago_start_expr) & (start_dt < current_month_start_expr)
).CALCULATE(
    treatment_start_month = DATETIME(start_dt, 'start of month'),
    # patient_id is needed for NDISTINCT to calculate PMPD
    # It's part of Treatments, but naming it explicitly for clarity in the PARTITION step.
    patient_identifier = patient_id
)

# Partition the filtered treatments by the calculated month
# Then, for each month, calculate PMPD and PMTC
monthly_diagnoses_treatments = treatments_in_period.PARTITION(
    name="treatments_by_month", by=(treatment_start_month)
).CALCULATE(
    # The month for which the stats are calculated
    month_of_treatment = treatment_start_month,
    # PMPD: Per Month Patient Diagnoses (count of unique patients with diagnoses/treatments)
    PMPD = NDISTINCT(treatments_in_period.patient_identifier),
    # PMTC: Per Month Treatment Count (total count of treatments)
    PMTC = COUNT(treatments_in_period)
)

# Order the results by month
final_result = monthly_diagnoses_treatments.ORDER_BY(month_of_treatment.ASC())
